becomes longer, it is more likely to contain minor logical errors, as
![](data:image/svg+xml;base64,PHN2ZyBpZD0iQTQucDEucGljNyIgY2xhc3M9Imx0eF9waWN0dXJlIiBoZWlnaHQ9IjEiIG92ZXJmbG93PSJ2aXNpYmxlIiB2ZXJzaW9uPSIxLjEiIHZpZXdib3g9IjAgMCAxIDEiIHdpZHRoPSIxIj48ZyBzdHlsZT0iLS1sdHgtc3Ryb2tlLWNvbG9yOiMwMDAwMDA7LS1sdHgtZmlsbC1jb2xvcjojMDAwMDAwOyIgZmlsbD0iIzAwMDAwMCIgc3Ryb2tlPSIjMDAwMDAwIiBzdHJva2Utd2lkdGg9IjAuNHB0IiB0cmFuc2Zvcm09InRyYW5zbGF0ZSgwLDEpIG1hdHJpeCgxIDAgMCAtMSAwIDApIj48ZyBzdHlsZT0iLS1sdHgtZmlsbC1jb2xvcjojRkZEOUQ5OyIgZmlsbD0iI0ZGRDlEOSI+PHBhdGggc3R5bGU9InN0cm9rZTpub25lIiBkPSJNIDY0LjcxIDEwLjk5IEwgMi41NSAxMC45OSBDIDAuMzggMTAuOTkgLTEuMzggOS4yMyAtMS4zOCA3LjA2IEwgLTEuMzggLTAuMTQgQyAtMS4zOCAtMi4zMSAwLjM4IC00LjA3IDIuNTUgLTQuMDcgTCA2NC43MSAtNC4wNyBDIDY2Ljg4IC00LjA3IDY4LjY1IC0yLjMxIDY4LjY1IC0wLjE0IEwgNjguNjUgNy4wNiBDIDY4LjY1IDkuMjMgNjYuODggMTAuOTkgNjQuNzEgMTAuOTkgWiBNIC0xLjM4IC00LjA3IiAvPjwvZz48ZyBzdHlsZT0iLS1sdHgtc3Ryb2tlLWNvbG9yOiMwMDAwMDA7LS1sdHgtZmlsbC1jb2xvcjojMDAwMDAwOyIgZmlsbD0iIzAwMDAwMCIgc3Ryb2tlPSIjMDAwMDAwIiB0cmFuc2Zvcm09Im1hdHJpeCgxLjAgMC4wIDAuMCAxLjAgMCAwKSI+PGZvcmVpZ25vYmplY3Qgc3R5bGU9Ii0tbHR4LWZvLXdpZHRoOjQuODllbTstLWx0eC1mby1oZWlnaHQ6MC42OWVtOy0tbHR4LWZvLWRlcHRoOjAuMTllbTtmb250LXNpemU6MTBwdDsiIGhlaWdodD0iMTIuMyIgb3ZlcmZsb3c9InZpc2libGUiIHRyYW5zZm9ybT0ibWF0cml4KDEgMCAwIC0xIDAgOS42MSkiIHdpZHRoPSI2Ny42NSI+PHNwYW4gY2xhc3M9Imx0eF9mb3JlaWdub2JqZWN0X2NvbnRhaW5lciI+PHNwYW4gY2xhc3M9Imx0eF9mb3JlaWdub2JqZWN0X2NvbnRlbnQiPjxzcGFuIGlkPSJBNC5wMS5waWM3LjEiIGNsYXNzPSJsdHhfdGV4dCI+aGlnaGxpZ2h0ZWQ8L3NwYW4+PC9zcGFuPjwvc3Bhbj48L2ZvcmVpZ25vYmplY3Q+PC9nPjwvZz48L3N2Zz4=)highlighted
in the rationales of Examples 3 and 4.

Besides minor logical errors in CoT reasoning, we also identify several
more pronounced error modes. First, even if the NL rationale looks
valid, the actual code fix might be incorrect (Example 4). Second, for
cases where there are multiple bugs in the program, the model might only
focus on the more obvious bugs during reasoning (e.g.,
![](data:image/svg+xml;base64,PHN2ZyBpZD0iQTQucDIucGljMSIgY2xhc3M9Imx0eF9waWN0dXJlIiBoZWlnaHQ9IjEiIG92ZXJmbG93PSJ2aXNpYmxlIiB2ZXJzaW9uPSIxLjEiIHZpZXdib3g9IjAgMCAxIDEiIHdpZHRoPSIxIj48ZyBzdHlsZT0iLS1sdHgtc3Ryb2tlLWNvbG9yOiMwMDAwMDA7LS1sdHgtZmlsbC1jb2xvcjojMDAwMDAwOyIgZmlsbD0iIzAwMDAwMCIgc3Ryb2tlPSIjMDAwMDAwIiBzdHJva2Utd2lkdGg9IjAuNHB0IiB0cmFuc2Zvcm09InRyYW5zbGF0ZSgwLDEpIG1hdHJpeCgxIDAgMCAtMSAwIDApIj48ZyBzdHlsZT0iLS1sdHgtZmlsbC1jb2xvcjojRDlGRkZGOyIgZmlsbD0iI0Q5RkZGRiI+PHBhdGggc3R5bGU9InN0cm9rZTpub25lIiBkPSJNIDIxOS4zNCAxMC45OSBMIDIuNTUgMTAuOTkgQyAwLjM4IDEwLjk5IC0xLjM4IDkuMjMgLTEuMzggNy4wNiBMIC0xLjM4IC0wLjE0IEMgLTEuMzggLTIuMzEgMC4zOCAtNC4wNyAyLjU1IC00LjA3IEwgMjE5LjM0IC00LjA3IEMgMjIxLjUxIC00LjA3IDIyMy4yOCAtMi4zMSAyMjMuMjggLTAuMTQgTCAyMjMuMjggNy4wNiBDIDIyMy4yOCA5LjIzIDIyMS41MSAxMC45OSAyMTkuMzQgMTAuOTkgWiBNIC0xLjM4IC00LjA3IiAvPjwvZz48ZyBzdHlsZT0iLS1sdHgtc3Ryb2tlLWNvbG9yOiMwMDAwMDA7LS1sdHgtZmlsbC1jb2xvcjojMDAwMDAwOyIgZmlsbD0iIzAwMDAwMCIgc3Ryb2tlPSIjMDAwMDAwIiB0cmFuc2Zvcm09Im1hdHJpeCgxLjAgMC4wIDAuMCAxLjAgMCAwKSI+PGZvcmVpZ25vYmplY3Qgc3R5bGU9Ii0tbHR4LWZvLXdpZHRoOjE1LjI3ZW07LS1sdHgtZm8taGVpZ2h0OjAuNjZlbTstLWx0eC1mby1kZXB0aDowLjE5ZW07Zm9udC1zaXplOjEwLjVwdDsiIGhlaWdodD0iMTIuMyIgb3ZlcmZsb3c9InZpc2libGUiIHRyYW5zZm9ybT0ibWF0cml4KDEgMCAwIC0xIDAgOS42MSkiIHdpZHRoPSIyMjEuODkiPjxzcGFuIGNsYXNzPSJsdHhfZm9yZWlnbm9iamVjdF9jb250YWluZXIiPjxzcGFuIGNsYXNzPSJsdHhfZm9yZWlnbm9iamVjdF9jb250ZW50Ij48c3BhbiBpZD0iQTQucDIucGljMS4xIiBjbGFzcz0ibHR4X3RleHQgbHR4X2ZvbnRfdXByaWdodCI+ZXhwbGFpbmluZyB0aGUgcmVhc29uIG9mIDxzcGFuIGlkPSJBNC5wMi5waWMxLjEuMSIgY2xhc3M9Imx0eF90ZXh0IGx0eF9mb250X3R5cGV3cml0ZXIiPkluZGV4RXJyb3I8L3NwYW4+PC9zcGFuPjwvc3Bhbj48L3NwYW4+PC9mb3JlaWdub2JqZWN0PjwvZz48L2c+PC9zdmc+)explaining

![](data:image/svg+xml;base64,PHN2ZyBpZD0iQTQucDIucGljMiIgY2xhc3M9Imx0eF9waWN0dXJlIiBoZWlnaHQ9IjEiIG92ZXJmbG93PSJ2aXNpYmxlIiB2ZXJzaW9uPSIxLjEiIHZpZXdib3g9IjAgMCAxIDEiIHdpZHRoPSIxIj48ZyBzdHlsZT0iLS1sdHgtc3Ryb2tlLWNvbG9yOiMwMDAwMDA7LS1sdHgtZmlsbC1jb2xvcjojMDAwMDAwOyIgZmlsbD0iIzAwMDAwMCIgc3Ryb2tlPSIjMDAwMDAwIiBzdHJva2Utd2lkdGg9IjAuNHB0IiB0cmFuc2Zvcm09InRyYW5zbGF0ZSgwLDEpIG1hdHJpeCgxIDAgMCAtMSAwIDApIj48ZyBzdHlsZT0iLS1sdHgtZmlsbC1jb2xvcjojRkZEOUQ5OyIgZmlsbD0iI0ZGRDlEOSI+PHBhdGggc3R5bGU9InN0cm9rZTpub25lIiBkPSJNIDEwMi41MyAxMC44NCBMIDIuNTUgMTAuODQgQyAwLjM4IDEwLjg0IC0xLjM4IDkuMDggLTEuMzggNi45IEwgLTEuMzggLTAuMTQgQyAtMS4zOCAtMi4zMSAwLjM4IC00LjA3IDIuNTUgLTQuMDcgTCAxMDIuNTMgLTQuMDcgQyAxMDQuNzEgLTQuMDcgMTA2LjQ3IC0yLjMxIDEwNi40NyAtMC4xNCBMIDEwNi40NyA2LjkgQyAxMDYuNDcgOS4wOCAxMDQuNzEgMTAuODQgMTAyLjUzIDEwLjg0IFogTSAtMS4zOCAtNC4wNyIgLz48L2c+PGcgc3R5bGU9Ii0tbHR4LXN0cm9rZS1jb2xvcjojMDAwMDAwOy0tbHR4LWZpbGwtY29sb3I6IzAwMDAwMDsiIGZpbGw9IiMwMDAwMDAiIHN0cm9rZT0iIzAwMDAwMCIgdHJhbnNmb3JtPSJtYXRyaXgoMS4wIDAuMCAwLjAgMS4wIDAgMCkiPjxmb3JlaWdub2JqZWN0IHN0eWxlPSItLWx0eC1mby13aWR0aDo3LjU5ZW07LS1sdHgtZm8taGVpZ2h0OjAuNjhlbTstLWx0eC1mby1kZXB0aDowLjE5ZW07Zm9udC1zaXplOjEwcHQ7IiBoZWlnaHQ9IjEyLjE1IiBvdmVyZmxvdz0idmlzaWJsZSIgdHJhbnNmb3JtPSJtYXRyaXgoMSAwIDAgLTEgMCA5LjQ2KSIgd2lkdGg9IjEwNS4wOSI+PHNwYW4gY2xhc3M9Imx0eF9mb3JlaWdub2JqZWN0X2NvbnRhaW5lciI+PHNwYW4gY2xhc3M9Imx0eF9mb3JlaWdub2JqZWN0X2NvbnRlbnQiPjxzcGFuIGlkPSJBNC5wMi5waWMyLjEiIGNsYXNzPSJsdHhfdGV4dCBsdHhfZm9udF91cHJpZ2h0Ij5JbmNvcnJlY3Qgb3V0cHV0czwvc3Bhbj48L3NwYW4+PC9zcGFuPjwvZm9yZWlnbm9iamVjdD48L2c+PC9nPjwvc3ZnPg==)Incorrect
outputs in Trace 1 and Trace 3 caused by zero-initialized freq_arr).
This could be especially problematic when there are hidden bugs _not_
captured by the trace, which could happen when a buggy program crashes
in the middle of execution and the rest the of the code is not covered
in the trace. Since the model could be more reliant on trace information
after fine-tuning, it might be more likely to miss such implicit bugs.

| Task: Write a function to remove leading zeroes from an ip address | Buggy Code: | [⬇](data:text/plain;base64,ZGVmIHJlbW92ZXplcm9faXAoaXApOgogICAgaXBfbGlzdCA9IGlwLnNwbGl0KCIuIikKICAgIGZvciBpIGluIHJhbmdlKDQpOgogICAgICAgIGlwX2xpc3RbaV0gPSBzdHIoaW50KGlwX2xpc3RbaV0pKQogICAgcmV0dXJuICIuIi5qb2luKGlwX2xpc3Qp) def removezero_ip(ip): ip_list = ip.split(".") for i in range(4): ip_list\[i\] = str(int(ip_list\[i\])) return ".".join(ip_list) | [⬇](data:text/plain;base64,VGhlIHByb2JsZW0gc3BlYyBhc2tzIHRvIHdyaXRlIGEgZnVuY3Rpb24gdG8gcmVtb3ZlIGxlYWRpbmcgemVyb2VzIGZyb20gYW4gSVAgYWRkcmVzcy4KCk5vdyBsZXQncyBsb29rIGF0IHRoZSBwcm9ncmFtLiBUaGUgcHJvZ3JhbSBmaXJzdCBzcGxpdHMgdGhlIElQIGFkZHJlc3MgaW50byBhIGxpc3Qgb2Ygc3RyaW5ncywgYW5kIHRoZW4gaXRlcmF0ZXMgdGhyb3VnaCB0aGUgbGlzdCB0byBjb252ZXJ0IGVhY2ggc3RyaW5nIHRvIGFuIGludGVnZXIsIGFuZCB0aGVuIGJhY2sgdG8gYSBzdHJpbmcuIFRoaXMgd2lsbCByZW1vdmUgbGVhZGluZyB6ZXJvZXMuICpAXGdvb2RyYXRzcGFue0hvd2V2ZXIsIHRoZSBwcm9ibGVtIGlzIHRoYXQgaXQgaXRlcmF0ZXMgdGhyb3VnaCB0aGUgbGlzdCBmb3IgNCB0aW1lcywgYnV0IHRoZSBsZW5ndGggb2YgdGhlfUAqICAqQFxnb29kcmF0c3BhbnsgbGlzdCBtaWdodCBub3QgYmUgNC4gRm9yIGV4YW1wbGUsIHdoZW4gYGlwPSIxMi4wMS4wMjQiYCwgdGhlIGxlbmd0aCBvZiB0aGUgbGlzdCBpcyBvbmx5IDMuIFRodXMgd2hlbiBgaT0zYCx9QCogKkBcZ29vZHJhdHNwYW57IGl0IHdpbGwgdGhyb3cgYW4gYEluZGV4RXJyb3JgfUAqLgoKSGVyZSBpcyBhIHNpbXBsZSBmaXg6) The problem spec asks to write a function to remove leading zeroes from an IP address. Now let’s look at the program. The program first splits the IP address into a list of strings, and then iterates through the list to convert each string to an integer, and then back to a string. This will remove leading zeroes. [⬇](data:text/plain;base64,ZGVmIHJlbW92ZXplcm9faXAoaXApOgogICAgaXBfbGlzdCA9IGlwLnNwbGl0KCIuIikKICAgIGZvciBpIGluIHJhbmdlKGxlbihpcF9saXN0KSk6CiAgICAgICAgaXBfbGlzdFtpXSA9IHN0cihpbnQoaXBfbGlzdFtpXSkpLmxzdHJpcCgnMCcpCiAgICByZXR1cm4gIi4iLmpvaW4oaXBfbGlzdCk=) def removezero_ip(ip): ip_list = ip.split(".") for i in range(len(ip_list)): ip_list\[i\] = str(int(ip_list\[i\])).lstrip(’0’) return ".".join(ip_list) Now let’s look at the program. The program first sorts the list of lists by the length of each sublist, and then sorts each sublist. | [⬇](data:text/plain;base64,VGhlIHByb2JsZW0gc3BlYyBhc2tzIHRvIHdyaXRlIGEgZnVuY3Rpb24gdG8gZ2V0IHRoZSBzdW0gb2YgYWxsIGRpZ2l0cyBpbiBhIG5vbi1uZWdhdGl2ZSBpbnRlZ2VyLgoKTm93IGxldCdzIGxvb2sgYXQgdGhlIHByb2dyYW0uIFRoZSBwcm9ncmFtIHNlZW1zIHRvIGJlIGNvcnJlY3QgYXQgdGhlIGZpcnN0IGdsYW5jZS4gSXQgZmlyc3QgaW5pdGlhbGl6ZXMgdGhlIHN1bSB0byAwLCBhbmQgdGhlbiBpdCB1c2VzIGEgd2hpbGUgbG9vcCB0byBnZXQgZWFjaCBkaWdpdCBpbiB0aGUgbnVtYmVyIGFuZCBhZGQgaXQgdG8gdGhlIHN1bS4gSG93ZXZlciwgaWYgd2UgbG9vayBhdCB0aGUgb3V0cHV0IG9mIHRoZSBwcm9ncmFtLCB3ZSB3aWxsIGZpbmQgdGhhdCB0aGUgc3VtIGlzIG5vdCBhbiBpbnRlZ2VyLiAqQFxnb29kcmF0c3BhbntUaGlzIGlzIGJlY2F1c2UgdGhlIHByb2dyYW0gZG9lcyBub3QgY29udmVydCB0aGUgbnVtYmVyIHRvIGFuIGludGVnZXIgYmVmb3JlIGl0IGdldHMgdGhlIGRpZ2l0cy4gRm9yIGV4YW1wbGUsfUAqICpAXGdvb2RyYXRzcGFueyBmb3IgdGhlIGZpcnN0IGFzc2VydGlvbiwgd2hlbiBgbnVtYmVyPTM0NWAsIHRoZSBmaXJzdCBkaWdpdCBgNWAgaXMgYWRkZWQgdG8gdGhlIHN1bSBhbmQgYHN1bT01YC4gVGhlbiBgbnVtYmVyYH1AKiAqQFxnb29kcmF0c3BhbntpcyB1cGRhdGVkIHRvIGAzNC41YCx9QCogKkBcYmFkcmF0c3BhbnthbmQgdGhlIHNlY29uZCBkaWdpdCBgNGAgaXMgYWRkZWQgdG8gdGhlIHN1bX1AKiAqQFxnb29kcmF0c3BhbnthbmQgYHN1bT05LjVgLiBBbmQgc28gb259QCouCgpIZXJlIGlzIGEgZml4ZWQgdmVyc2lvbjo=) The problem spec asks to write a function to get the sum of all digits in a non-negative integer. Now let’s look at the program. The program seems to be correct at the first glance. It first initializes the sum to 0, and then it uses a while loop to get each digit in the number and add it to the sum. However, if we look at the output of the program, we will find that the sum is not an integer. ![](data:image/svg+xml;base64,PHN2ZyBpZD0ibHN0bnVtYmVyeDE0MS5waWMxIiBjbGFzcz0ibHR4X3BpY3R1cmUiIGhlaWdodD0iMSIgb3ZlcmZsb3c9InZpc2libGUiIHZlcnNpb249IjEuMSIgdmlld2JveD0iMCAwIDEgMSIgd2lkdGg9IjEiPjxnIHN0eWxlPSItLWx0eC1zdHJva2UtY29sb3I6Izk0MDBEMTstLWx0eC1maWxsLWNvbG9yOiM5NDAwRDE7IiBmaWxsPSIjOTQwMEQxIiBzdHJva2U9IiM5NDAwRDEiIHN0cm9rZS13aWR0aD0iMC40cHQiIHRyYW5zZm9ybT0idHJhbnNsYXRlKDAsMSkgbWF0cml4KDEgMCAwIC0xIDAgMCkiPjxnIHN0eWxlPSItLWx0eC1maWxsLWNvbG9yOiNEOUZGRkY7IiBmaWxsPSIjRDlGRkZGIj48cGF0aCBzdHlsZT0ic3Ryb2tlOm5vbmUiIGQ9Ik0gNTUxLjcyIDcuMyBMIDIuNTUgNy4zIEMgMC4zOCA3LjMgLTEuMzggNS41NCAtMS4zOCAzLjM3IEwgLTEuMzggMC40IEMgLTEuMzggLTEuNzcgMC4zOCAtMy41NCAyLjU1IC0zLjU0IEwgNTUxLjcyIC0zLjU0IEMgNTUzLjg5IC0zLjU0IDU1NS42NSAtMS43NyA1NTUuNjUgMC40IEwgNTU1LjY1IDMuMzcgQyA1NTUuNjUgNS41NCA1NTMuODkgNy4zIDU1MS43MiA3LjMgWiBNIC0xLjM4IC0zLjU0IiAvPjwvZz48ZyBzdHlsZT0iLS1sdHgtc3Ryb2tlLWNvbG9yOiM5NDAwRDE7LS1sdHgtZmlsbC1jb2xvcjojOTQwMEQxOyIgZmlsbD0iIzk0MDBEMSIgc3Ryb2tlPSIjOTQwMEQxIiB0cmFuc2Zvcm09Im1hdHJpeCgxLjAgMC4wIDAuMCAxLjAgMCAwKSI+PGZvcmVpZ25vYmplY3Qgc3R5bGU9Ii0tbHR4LWZvLXdpZHRoOjU0LjVlbTstLWx0eC1mby1oZWlnaHQ6MC41OGVtOy0tbHR4LWZvLWRlcHRoOjAuMjFlbTtmb250LXNpemU6Ny4zNXB0OyIgaGVpZ2h0PSI4LjA3IiBvdmVyZmxvdz0idmlzaWJsZSIgdHJhbnNmb3JtPSJtYXRyaXgoMSAwIDAgLTEgMCA1LjkyKSIgd2lkdGg9IjU1NC4yOCI+PHNwYW4gY2xhc3M9Imx0eF9mb3JlaWdub2JqZWN0X2NvbnRhaW5lciI+PHNwYW4gY2xhc3M9Imx0eF9mb3JlaWdub2JqZWN0X2NvbnRlbnQiPjxzcGFuIGlkPSJsc3RudW1iZXJ4MTQxLnBpYzEuMSIgY2xhc3M9Imx0eF90ZXh0IGx0eF9mb250X3R5cGV3cml0ZXIiPlRoaXMgaXMgYmVjYXVzZSB0aGUgcHJvZ3JhbSBkb2VzIG5vdCBjb252ZXJ0IHRoZSBudW1iZXIgdG8gYW4gaW50ZWdlciBiZWZvcmUgaXQgZ2V0cyB0aGUgZGlnaXRzLiBGb3IgZXhhbXBsZSw8L3NwYW4+PC9zcGFuPjwvc3Bhbj48L2ZvcmVpZ25vYmplY3Q+PC9nPjwvZz48L3N2Zz4=)This is because the program does not convert the number to an integer before it gets the digits. ‘sum=9.5‘. And so on. Here is a fixed version:’ |
| [⬇](data:text/plain;base64,ZGVmIHN1bV9kaWdpdHMobnVtYmVyKToKICAgIG51bWJlciA9IGludChudW1iZXIpCiAgICBzdW0gPSAwCiAgICB3aGlsZShudW1iZXIpOgogICAgICAgIHN1bSArPSBudW1iZXIgJQogICAgICAgIG51bWJlciA9IG51bWJlciAvLyAxMAogICAgcmV0dXJuIHN1bQ==) def sum_digits(number): number = int(number) sum = 0 while(number): sum += number % number = number // 10 return sum | Example 4: An example where PaLM 2-L$`+`$NExT reasons over multiple steps of execution | Task: Write a function to convert tuple into list by adding the given string after every element. | Buggy Code: | [⬇](data:text/plain;base64,ZGVmIGFkZF9zdHIodGVzdF90dXBsZSwgYWRkX3N0cik6CiAgICByZXMgPSBsaXN0KHRlc3RfdHVwbGUpCiAgICBmb3IgaSBpbiByYW5nZSgxLGxlbih0ZXN0X3R1cGxlKSk6CiAgICAgICAgcmVzLmluc2VydChpKjItMSxhZGRfc3RyKQogICAgcmV0dXJuIHJlcw==) def add_str(test_tuple, add_str): res = list(test_tuple) for i in range(1,len(test_tuple)): res.insert(i\*2-1,add_str) return res Now let’s look at the program. The program first converts the tuple to a list, which is good. Then it iterates from 1 to the length of the list, and inserts the given string at index ‘i\*2-1‘. ![](data:image/svg+xml;base64,PHN2ZyBpZD0ibHN0bnVtYmVyeDE5MS5waWM2IiBjbGFzcz0ibHR4X3BpY3R1cmUiIGhlaWdodD0iMSIgb3ZlcmZsb3c9InZpc2libGUiIHZlcnNpb249IjEuMSIgdmlld2JveD0iMCAwIDEgMSIgd2lkdGg9IjEiPjxnIHN0eWxlPSItLWx0eC1zdHJva2UtY29sb3I6Izk0MDBEMTstLWx0eC1maWxsLWNvbG9yOiM5NDAwRDE7IiBmaWxsPSIjOTQwMEQxIiBzdHJva2U9IiM5NDAwRDEiIHN0cm9rZS13aWR0aD0iMC40cHQiIHRyYW5zZm9ybT0idHJhbnNsYXRlKDAsMSkgbWF0cml4KDEgMCAwIC0xIDAgMCkiPjxnIHN0eWxlPSItLWx0eC1maWxsLWNvbG9yOiNEOUZGRkY7IiBmaWxsPSIjRDlGRkZGIj48cGF0aCBzdHlsZT0ic3Ryb2tlOm5vbmUiIGQ9Ik0gNDA5LjMzIDguMTEgTCAyLjU1IDguMTEgQyAwLjM4IDguMTEgLTEuMzggNi4zNSAtMS4zOCA0LjE3IEwgLTEuMzggMC40IEMgLTEuMzggLTEuNzcgMC4zOCAtMy41NCAyLjU1IC0zLjU0IEwgNDA5LjMzIC0zLjU0IEMgNDExLjUxIC0zLjU0IDQxMy4yNyAtMS43NyA0MTMuMjcgMC40IEwgNDEzLjI3IDQuMTcgQyA0MTMuMjcgNi4zNSA0MTEuNTEgOC4xMSA0MDkuMzMgOC4xMSBaIE0gLTEuMzggLTMuNTQiIC8+PC9nPjxnIHN0eWxlPSItLWx0eC1zdHJva2UtY29sb3I6Izk0MDBEMTstLWx0eC1maWxsLWNvbG9yOiM5NDAwRDE7IiBmaWxsPSIjOTQwMEQxIiBzdHJva2U9IiM5NDAwRDEiIHRyYW5zZm9ybT0ibWF0cml4KDEuMCAwLjAgMC4wIDEuMCAwIDApIj48Zm9yZWlnbm9iamVjdCBzdHlsZT0iLS1sdHgtZm8td2lkdGg6NDAuNWVtOy0tbHR4LWZvLWhlaWdodDowLjY2ZW07LS1sdHgtZm8tZGVwdGg6MC4yMWVtO2ZvbnQtc2l6ZTo3LjM1cHQ7IiBoZWlnaHQ9IjguODgiIG92ZXJmbG93PSJ2aXNpYmxlIiB0cmFuc2Zvcm09Im1hdHJpeCgxIDAgMCAtMSAwIDYuNzMpIiB3aWR0aD0iNDExLjg5Ij48c3BhbiBjbGFzcz0ibHR4X2ZvcmVpZ25vYmplY3RfY29udGFpbmVyIj48c3BhbiBjbGFzcz0ibHR4X2ZvcmVpZ25vYmplY3RfY29udGVudCI+PHNwYW4gaWQ9ImxzdG51bWJlcngxOTEucGljNi4xIiBjbGFzcz0ibHR4X3RleHQgbHR4X2ZvbnRfdHlwZXdyaXRlciI+VGhlIHN0cmluZyB3aWxsIG5vdCBiZSBpbnNlcnRlZCBhZnRlciB0aGUgbGFzdCBlbGVtZW50IChpLmUuLCB0aGUgbmludGggZWxlbWVudCkuPC9zcGFuPjwvc3Bhbj48L3NwYW4+PC9mb3JlaWdub2JqZWN0PjwvZz48L2c+PC9zdmc+)The string will not be inserted after the last element (i.e., the ninth element). Here is a simple fix:’ |
| [⬇](data:text/plain;base64,ZGVmIGFkZF9zdHIodGVzdF90dXBsZSwgYWRkX3N0cik6CiAgICByZXMgPSBsaXN0KHRlc3RfdHVwbGUpCiAgICBmb3IgaSBpbiByYW5nZShsZW4odGVzdF90dXBsZSkpOgogICAgICAgIHJlcy5pbnNlcnQoaSoyLGFkZF9zdHIpCiAgICByZXR1cm4gcmVz) def add_str(test_tuple, add_str): res = list(test_tuple) for i in range(len(test_tuple)): res.insert(i\*2,add_str) return res ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Example 5: An example where PaLM 2-L$`+`$NExT failed to identify all the bugs in the program                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Task: Write a python function to find the difference between highest and least frequencies in a given array.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| Buggy Code:                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| [⬇](data:text/plain;base64,ZGVmIGZpbmRfRGlmZihudW1zLCBuKToKICAgIGZyZXFfYXJyID0gWzAgZm9yIGkgaW4gcmFuZ2UobildCiAgICBmb3IgaSBpbiByYW5nZShsZW4obnVtcykpOgogICAgICAgIGZyZXFfYXJyW251bXNbaV1dID0gZnJlcV9hcnJbbnVtc1tpXV0gKyAxCiAgICByZXR1cm4gbWF4KGZyZXFfYXJyKSAtIG1pbihmcmVxX2Fycik=) def find_Diff(nums, n): freq_arr = \[0 for i in range(n)\] for i in range(len(nums)): freq_arr\[nums\[i\]\] = freq_arr\[nums\[i\]\] + 1 return max(freq_arr) - min(freq_arr)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| Test Cases: [⬇](data:text/plain;base64,YXNzZXJ0IGZpbmRfRGlmZihbMSwxLDIsMiw3LDgsNCw1LDEsNF0sMTApID09IDIKYXNzZXJ0IGZpbmRfRGlmZihbMSw3LDksMiwzLDMsMSwzLDNdLDkpID09IDMKYXNzZXJ0IGZpbmRfRGlmZihbMSwyLDEsMl0sNCkgPT0gMA==) assert find_Diff(\[1,1,2,2,7,8,4,5,1,4\],10) == 2 assert find_Diff(\[1,7,9,2,3,3,1,3,3\],9) == 3 assert find_Diff(\[1,2,1,2\],4) == 0 Now let’s look at the program. The program first creates a frequency array of length ‘n‘, and then iterates through the input array to count the frequency of each number. It then returns the difference between the maximum and the minimum frequencies in the frequency array. The program looks good at the first glance. However, the numbers in the input array can be larger than ‘n‘, and thus the frequency array will not be able ![](data:image/svg+xml;base64,PHN2ZyBpZD0ibHN0bnVtYmVyeDIzNy5waWMzIiBjbGFzcz0ibHR4X3BpY3R1cmUiIGhlaWdodD0iMSIgb3ZlcmZsb3c9InZpc2libGUiIHZlcnNpb249IjEuMSIgdmlld2JveD0iMCAwIDEgMSIgd2lkdGg9IjEiPjxnIHN0eWxlPSItLWx0eC1zdHJva2UtY29sb3I6Izk0MDBEMTstLWx0eC1maWxsLWNvbG9yOiM5NDAwRDE7IiBmaWxsPSIjOTQwMEQxIiBzdHJva2U9IiM5NDAwRDEiIHN0cm9rZS13aWR0aD0iMC40cHQiIHRyYW5zZm9ybT0idHJhbnNsYXRlKDAsMSkgbWF0cml4KDEgMCAwIC0xIDAgMCkiPjxnIHN0eWxlPSItLWx0eC1maWxsLWNvbG9yOiNEOUZGRkY7IiBmaWxsPSIjRDlGRkZGIj48cGF0aCBzdHlsZT0ic3Ryb2tlOm5vbmUiIGQ9Ik0gMjUxLjcgNy4zIEwgMi41NSA3LjMgQyAwLjM4IDcuMyAtMS4zOCA1LjU0IC0xLjM4IDMuMzcgTCAtMS4zOCAwLjQgQyAtMS4zOCAtMS43NyAwLjM4IC0zLjU0IDIuNTUgLTMuNTQgTCAyNTEuNyAtMy41NCBDIDI1My44NyAtMy41NCAyNTUuNjQgLTEuNzcgMjU1LjY0IDAuNCBMIDI1NS42NCAzLjM3IEMgMjU1LjY0IDUuNTQgMjUzLjg3IDcuMyAyNTEuNyA3LjMgWiBNIC0xLjM4IC0zLjU0IiAvPjwvZz48ZyBzdHlsZT0iLS1sdHgtc3Ryb2tlLWNvbG9yOiM5NDAwRDE7LS1sdHgtZmlsbC1jb2xvcjojOTQwMEQxOyIgZmlsbD0iIzk0MDBEMSIgc3Ryb2tlPSIjOTQwMEQxIiB0cmFuc2Zvcm09Im1hdHJpeCgxLjAgMC4wIDAuMCAxLjAgMCAwKSI+PGZvcmVpZ25vYmplY3Qgc3R5bGU9Ii0tbHR4LWZvLXdpZHRoOjI1ZW07LS1sdHgtZm8taGVpZ2h0OjAuNThlbTstLWx0eC1mby1kZXB0aDowLjIxZW07Zm9udC1zaXplOjcuMzVwdDsiIGhlaWdodD0iOC4wNyIgb3ZlcmZsb3c9InZpc2libGUiIHRyYW5zZm9ybT0ibWF0cml4KDEgMCAwIC0xIDAgNS45MikiIHdpZHRoPSIyNTQuMjUiPjxzcGFuIGNsYXNzPSJsdHhfZm9yZWlnbm9iamVjdF9jb250YWluZXIiPjxzcGFuIGNsYXNzPSJsdHhfZm9yZWlnbm9iamVjdF9jb250ZW50Ij48c3BhbiBpZD0ibHN0bnVtYmVyeDIzNy5waWMzLjEiIGNsYXNzPSJsdHhfdGV4dCBsdHhfZm9udF90eXBld3JpdGVyIj50byBhY2NvbW1vZGF0ZSBhbGwgdGhlIG51bWJlcnMgaW4gdGhlIGlucHV0IGFycmF5Ljwvc3Bhbj48L3NwYW4+PC9zcGFuPjwvZm9yZWlnbm9iamVjdD48L2c+PC9nPjwvc3ZnPg==) to accommodate all the numbers in the input array. For example, in the second assertion, the input array is ‘\[1, 7, 9, 2, 3, 3, 1, 3, 3\]‘, and the length of the frequency array is also ‘9‘. When ‘nums\[i\]=9‘, it will try to access ‘freq_arr\[9\]‘, which is out of bound, and thus an ‘IndexError‘ is thrown. Here is a simple fix:’                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| [⬇](data:text/plain;base64,ZGVmIGZpbmRfRGlmZihudW1zLCBuKToKICAgIGZyZXFfYXJyID0ge30KICAgIGZvciBpIGluIHJhbmdlKGxlbihudW1zKSk6CiAgICAgICAgaWYgbnVtc1tpXSBpbiBmcmVxX2FycjoKICAgICAgICAgICAgZnJlcV9hcnJbbnVtc1tpXV0gKz0gMQogICAgICAgIGVsc2U6CiAgICAgICAgICAgIGZyZXFfYXJyW251bXNbaV1dID0gMQogICAgbWF4X2ZyZXEgPSAwCiAgICBtaW5fZnJlcSA9IDAKICAgIGZvciBudW0sIGZyZXEgaW4gZnJlcV9hcnIuaXRlbXMoKToKICAgICAgICBpZiBmcmVxID4gbWF4X2ZyZXE6CiAgICAgICAgICAgIG1heF9mcmVxID0gZnJlcQogICAgICAgIGlmIGZyZXEgPCBtaW5fZnJlcSBvciBtaW5fZnJlcSA9PSAwOgogICAgICAgICAgICBtaW5fZnJlcSA9IGZyZXEKICAgIHJldHVybiBtYXhfZnJlcSAtIG1pbl9mcmVx) def find_Diff(nums, n): freq_arr = {} for i in range(len(nums)): if nums\[i\] in freq_arr: freq_arr\[nums\[i\]\] += 1 else: freq_arr\[nums\[i\]\] = 1 max_freq = 0 min_freq = 0 for num, freq in freq_arr.items(): if freq \> max_freq: max_freq = freq if freq \< min_freq or min_freq == 0: min_freq = freq return max_freq - min_freq                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |

| Rationale and Code Solution: (code fix is wrong ![[Uncaptioned image]](2404.14662v1/x2.png) ) | [⬇](data:text/plain;base64,Tm93IGxldCdzIGxvb2sgYXQgdGhlIHByb2dyYW0uIFRoZSBwcm9ncmFtIGxvb2tzIGdvb2QgYXQgdGhlIGZpcnN0IGdsYW5jZSBhcyBgdGV4dC5maW5kKHBhdHRlcm4pYCBpcyB0aGUgY29ycmVjdCB3YXkgdG8gZmluZCB0aGUgbG9jYXRpb24gb2YgYSBsaXRlcmFsIHN0cmluZyBpbiBhIGdpdmVuIHN0cmluZy4gQnV0IHRoZSBwcm9ibGVtIGlzIHRoYXQgaXQgZG9lcyBub3QgdXNlIHRoZSBgdHVwbGUoKWAgZnVuY3Rpb24gY29ycmVjdGx5LiBUaGUgYHR1cGxlKClgIGZ1bmN0aW9uIHRha2VzIGEgc2luZ2xlIGl0ZXJhYmxlIGFzIGlucHV0LCBhbmQgcmV0dXJucyBhIHR1cGxlIHdpdGggdGhlIGVsZW1lbnRzIGZyb20gdGhlIGl0ZXJhYmxlLiAqQFxnb29kcmF0c3BhbntCdXQgaW4gdGhlIHByb2dyYW0sIGl0IHRha2VzIHR3byBhcmd1bWVudHMsIHdoaWNoIHdpbGwgdGhyb3cgYX1AKiAqQFxnb29kcmF0c3BhbnsgYFR5cGVFcnJvcmAgb2YgYHR1cGxlIGV4cGVjdGVkIGF0IG1vc3QgMSBhcmd1bWVudCwgZ290IDJgLn1AKgoKSGVyZSBpcyBhIHNpbXBsZSBmaXg6) Now let’s look at the program. The program looks good at the first glance as ‘text.find(pattern)‘ is the correct way to find the location of a literal string in a given string. But the problem is that it does not use the ‘tuple()‘ function correctly. The ‘tuple()‘ function takes a single iterable as input, and returns a tuple with the elements from the iterable. ![](data:image/svg+xml;base64,PHN2ZyBpZD0ibHN0bnVtYmVyeDI4NS5waWMxIiBjbGFzcz0ibHR4X3BpY3R1cmUiIGhlaWdodD0iMSIgb3ZlcmZsb3c9InZpc2libGUiIHZlcnNpb249IjEuMSIgdmlld2JveD0iMCAwIDEgMSIgd2lkdGg9IjEiPjxnIHN0eWxlPSItLWx0eC1zdHJva2UtY29sb3I6Izk0MDBEMTstLWx0eC1maWxsLWNvbG9yOiM5NDAwRDE7IiBmaWxsPSIjOTQwMEQxIiBzdHJva2U9IiM5NDAwRDEiIHN0cm9rZS13aWR0aD0iMC40cHQiIHRyYW5zZm9ybT0idHJhbnNsYXRlKDAsMSkgbWF0cml4KDEgMCAwIC0xIDAgMCkiPjxnIHN0eWxlPSItLWx0eC1maWxsLWNvbG9yOiNEOUZGRkY7IiBmaWxsPSIjRDlGRkZGIj48cGF0aCBzdHlsZT0ic3Ryb2tlOm5vbmUiIGQ9Ik0gMzEyLjcyIDcuMyBMIDIuNTUgNy4zIEMgMC4zOCA3LjMgLTEuMzggNS41NCAtMS4zOCAzLjM3IEwgLTEuMzggMC40IEMgLTEuMzggLTEuNzcgMC4zOCAtMy41NCAyLjU1IC0zLjU0IEwgMzEyLjcyIC0zLjU0IEMgMzE0Ljg5IC0zLjU0IDMxNi42NiAtMS43NyAzMTYuNjYgMC40IEwgMzE2LjY2IDMuMzcgQyAzMTYuNjYgNS41NCAzMTQuODkgNy4zIDMxMi43MiA3LjMgWiBNIC0xLjM4IC0zLjU0IiAvPjwvZz48ZyBzdHlsZT0iLS1sdHgtc3Ryb2tlLWNvbG9yOiM5NDAwRDE7LS1sdHgtZmlsbC1jb2xvcjojOTQwMEQxOyIgZmlsbD0iIzk0MDBEMSIgc3Ryb2tlPSIjOTQwMEQxIiB0cmFuc2Zvcm09Im1hdHJpeCgxLjAgMC4wIDAuMCAxLjAgMCAwKSI+PGZvcmVpZ25vYmplY3Qgc3R5bGU9Ii0tbHR4LWZvLXdpZHRoOjMxZW07LS1sdHgtZm8taGVpZ2h0OjAuNThlbTstLWx0eC1mby1kZXB0aDowLjIxZW07Zm9udC1zaXplOjcuMzVwdDsiIGhlaWdodD0iOC4wNyIgb3ZlcmZsb3c9InZpc2libGUiIHRyYW5zZm9ybT0ibWF0cml4KDEgMCAwIC0xIDAgNS45MikiIHdpZHRoPSIzMTUuMjgiPjxzcGFuIGNsYXNzPSJsdHhfZm9yZWlnbm9iamVjdF9jb250YWluZXIiPjxzcGFuIGNsYXNzPSJsdHhfZm9yZWlnbm9iamVjdF9jb250ZW50Ij48c3BhbiBpZD0ibHN0bnVtYmVyeDI4NS5waWMxLjEiIGNsYXNzPSJsdHhfdGV4dCBsdHhfZm9udF90eXBld3JpdGVyIj5CdXQgaW4gdGhlIHByb2dyYW0sIGl0IHRha2VzIHR3byBhcmd1bWVudHMsIHdoaWNoIHdpbGwgdGhyb3cgYTwvc3Bhbj48L3NwYW4+PC9zcGFuPjwvZm9yZWlnbm9iamVjdD48L2c+PC9nPjwvc3ZnPg==)But in the program, it takes two arguments, which will throw a ‘TypeError‘ of ‘tuple expected at most 1 argument, got 2‘. Here is a simple fix:’ |
| [⬇](data:text/plain;base64,ZGVmIHNlYXJjaF9saXRlcmFsKHBhdHRlcm4sIHRleHQpOgogICAgbWF0Y2ggPSB0dXBsZSh0ZXh0LmZpbmQocGF0dGVybiksKQogICAgbWF0Y2ggPSBtYXRjaCArICh0ZXh0LmZpbmQocGF0dGVybikgKyBsZW4ocGF0dGVybikgLSAxLCkKCiAgICByZXR1cm4gbWF0Y2g=) def search_literal(pattern, text): match = tuple(text.find(pattern),) match = match + (text.find(pattern) + len(pattern) - 1,) return match                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |

## Appendix E Full Prompt

this work. The rationales and fixes in the few-shot examples are hand
annotated by the authors. Each exemplar has three unit tests and their
traces.

We are doing pair programming, I will give you the problem definition
and my implementation, which does not pass all test cases. And you need
to help me to fix my code.

\# Example 1

Here is the specification in natural language and the program that I
wrote:

“‘

\# Write a function to find the similar elements from the given two
tuple lists.

def similar_elements(t1, t2):

s1 = set(t1)

s2 = set(t2)

return s1.intersection(s2)

“‘

Here are the assertions I need to pass:

“‘

assert similar_elements((3, 4, 5, 6),(5, 7, 4, 10)) == (4, 5)

assert similar_elements((1, 2, 3, 4),(5, 4, 3, 7)) == (3, 4)

assert similar_elements((11, 12, 14, 13),(17, 15, 14, 13)) == (13, 14)

“‘

Here is a \*cheatsheet\* for the gold execution trace for each
assertions, use the information here but pretend you did all the
reasoning about execution yourself.

\#################### cheatsheet starts \####################

def similar_elements(t1, t2): \# (0) t1=(3, 4, 5, 6), t2=(5, 7, 4, 10);

s1 = set(t1) \# (1) s1={3, 4, 5, 6};

s2 = set(t2) \# (2) s2={10, 4, 5, 7};

return s1.intersection(s2) \# (3) \_\_return\_\_={4, 5}

assert similar_elements((3, 4, 5, 6),(5, 7, 4, 10)) == (4, 5) \#

——————–

def similar_elements(t1, t2): \# (0) t1=(1, 2, 3, 4), t2=(5, 4, 3, 7);

s1 = set(t1) \# (1) s1={1, 2, 3, 4};

s2 = set(t2) \# (2) s2={3, 4, 5, 7};

return s1.intersection(s2) \# (3) \_\_return\_\_={3, 4}

assert similar_elements((1, 2, 3, 4),(5, 4, 3, 7)) == (3, 4) \#

——————–

def similar_elements(t1, t2): \# (0) t1=(11, 12, 14, 13), t2=(17, 15,
14, 13);

s1 = set(t1) \# (1) s1={11, 12, 13, 14};

s2 = set(t2) \# (2) s2={17, 13, 14, 15};

return s1.intersection(s2) \# (3) \_\_return\_\_={13, 14}

assert similar_elements((11, 12, 14, 13),(17, 15, 14, 13)) == (13, 14)
\#

\#################### cheatsheet ends \####################

Given the above information, can you tell me why my code is wrong, and
also provide a fixed version?

The problem spec asks to find the similar elements in the two tuples,
from the assertion we can see that it essentially wants the common
elements from the two tuples. It also seems that the common elements
should be returned in a tuple with ascending order.

Now let’s look at the program. While it is correct and efficient to use
‘intersection()‘ to find the common elements between two sets, it will
also return a set. Take the first assertion as an example, ‘s1‘ will
first be converted to a set like ‘{3, 4, 5, 6}‘ and similarly for ‘s2‘,
then the return value will be ‘{4, 5}‘. Though the elements are right,
the expected return value is a tuple and not a set. Thus we only need to
further convert the sets to tuples using ‘tuple()‘.

Here is the corrected code:

“‘

def similar_elements(t1, t2):

s1 = set(t1)

s2 = set(t2)

return tuple(s1.intersection(s2))

“‘

Given the above information, can you tell me why my code is wrong, and

The problem spec asks to write a Python function that identifies
non-prime numbers. Non-prime numbers are numbers that are divisible by
numbers other than 1 and itself. Note that 0 and 1 are also non-prime
numbers.

Now let’s check the program. Let’s run the program through some
examples. For the first assertion, when ‘n=2‘, it will enter the ‘else‘
branch, and then in the for loop, since the condition is ‘i in range(2,
n//2+1)‘, which is ‘range(2, 2)‘, it will actually not enter the loop
since the condition is not met in the first place. And then it will
reach the end of the function, and the return value will be ‘None‘. And
for the third assertion, when ‘n=35‘, it also enters the ‘else‘ branch,
and it then enters the loop with ‘i=2‘ as ‘n//2+1=18 \> 2‘. Then in the
next step, since ‘n%

While it is not obvious how to correct this program, another way to
implement the ‘is_not_prime()‘ function is just to first create a helper
function to identify prime numbers as ‘is_prime()‘, and then simply
‘return not is_prime(x)‘.

Here is a re-implementation:

“‘

def is_prime(x):

if x \<= 1:

return False

else:

for i in range(2,x):

if x%

return False

return True

def is_not_prime(x):

return not is_prime(x)

“‘

\# Example 3

Here is the specification in natural language and the program that I

“‘

\# Write a function to find the largest integers from a given list of
numbers using heap queue algorithm.

def heap_queue_largest(list1,n):

largest=heapq.nlargest(n,list1)

return largest

“‘

assert heap_queue_largest( \[25, 35, 22, 85, 14, 65, 75, 22,
58\],3)==\[85, 75, 65\]

58\],2)==\[85, 75\]

assert heap_queue_largest( \[25, 35, 22, 85, 14, 65, 75, 22,
58\],5)==\[85, 75, 65, 58, 35\]

“‘

assertions, use the information here but pretend you did all the
reasoning about execution yourself.

NameError("name ’heapq’ is not defined")

return largest

Given the above information, can you tell me why my code is wrong, and
also provide a fixed version?

The problem spec asks to find ‘n‘ largest integers in a given list. It
also suggests that the heap queue shall be used.

Now let’s look at the program. The program looks good at the first
glance as ‘heapq.nlargest()‘ is the correct way to get the ‘n‘ largest
integers from an iterable (e.g., a list). But the problem is that it
does not import the ‘heapq‘ package first. Thus all three assertions
will fail at the first line of the function, and throw a ‘NameError‘ of
‘name ’heapq’ is not defined‘.

Here is a simple fix:

“‘

import heapq

def heap_queue_largest(list1,n):

largest=heapq.nlargest(n,list1)

return largest

“‘
